## Document 041: 20261023【投研報告】市場快訊_AI資金壓力拖累科技類股，惟產業需求仍具韌性

AI 資金壓力拖累科技股，惟產業需求仍具韌性
2026 年 10 月 23 日
一、 市場動態
1. 費半再度重挫，科技股成為美股主要賣壓來源：美國股市主要指數 10 月 22 日跌多漲
少，道瓊工業指數上漲 0.02%，收 52,867.98 點；S&P 500 指數下跌 1.21%，收 7,663.43
點；Nasdaq 綜合指數下跌 2.60%，收 26,008.86 點，創 6 月 5 日以來最大單日跌幅
。費城半導體指數重挫 6.72%，收 10,731.98 點，跌幅明顯大於主要股價指數，顯示
AI 及半導體族群仍是本波市場修正的主要壓力來源。

2. AI 相關企業全面承壓，資金流向非科技股及公債：10 月 22 日 Meta 下跌約 7%、Lam
Research 跌幅逾一成，Nvidia 下跌 4.4%，其他 AI 相關股票亦明顯走低，顯示市場
對 AI 資本支出及投資報酬的疑慮已由大型科技平台延伸至半導體與基礎建設供應商
。不過，道瓊上漲 0.02%，羅素 2000 指數亦小幅收紅，反映資金並未全面撤出股市
；美國 10 年期公債殖利率則因避險需求跌破 5%，顯示資金由高評價科技股轉向其他
產業及避險資產。
3. 中東地緣政治風險升溫，油價攀至 5 月以來高點：中東能源運輸及供給風險 10 月 22
日再度升高，布蘭特原油價格上漲約 3.76%，升至每桶 113 美元附近，創 5 月以來高
點。油價攀升除使市場重新關注能源成本對通膨與 Fed 政策路徑的影響，也可能增加
AI 資料中心的電力及營運成本。中東情勢雖非本次半導體股重挫的唯一因素，但在市
場已擔憂 AI 投資效益的情況下，能源價格上揚進一步壓抑投資人風險偏好。
二、 下跌原因分析：

1. 殖利率回落難抵科技股下跌，顯示修正因素出現轉變：10 月 14 日費半重挫主要受到
美國 9 月 CPI 高於預期、升息疑慮及美債殖利率盤中急升影響，惟當日 10 年期殖利
率收盤反而小幅下降；10 月 22 日情況則更加明顯，美國 10 年期公債殖利率因避險
需求盤中跌破 5%，費半仍大跌 6.72%。這顯示市場關注焦點已由單純的利率與估值
壓力，逐步轉向 AI 資本支出的資金來源、融資能力及投資回收期。因此，即使後續殖
利率回落，若企業仍無法證明 AI 投資能轉化為足夠營收與現金流，科技股評價仍可能
持續受到壓抑。
2. Meta 自由現金流轉負，AI 投資成本由預期轉為實際財務壓力：Meta 第三季營收 646.0
億美元、年增 26%，顯示核心業務仍維持強勁成長；但資本支出達 349.1 億美元，超
過同期營業現金流 327.9 億美元，使自由現金流由去年同期正 106.3 億美元轉為負
21.2 億美元。公司亦將全年資本支出預估調整至 1,350～1,450 億美元，並宣布設立

無表決權 Class C 股票，增加未來資本運用彈性。這反映 AI 投資規模已開始超越部分

確性不做任何擔保，如有錯漏或疏忽，本行及本行之任何董事或受僱人，毋須負任何法律責任。任何人因信賴此等資料而做出或改變投資決
策，應審慎衡量本身之需求及投資風險，自行承擔一切投資風險並自負盈虧，本行不負任何法律責任。本報告受中華民國著作權法保護為元
大銀行所有，非經本行同意，不得將本報告內容之一部或全部加以複製、轉載或散布。本報告所載述的意見本行可隨時予以更改或撤回，恕
不另行通知。
大型科技企業當期現金創造能力，但 Meta 仍持有 824.5 億美元現金、約當現金及有
價證券，不能將單季自由現金流轉負直接解讀為財務危機。
3. 資本稀缺逐漸成為 AI 擴張瓶頸，新興雲端業者面臨更大壓力：AI 發展需要持續投入
GPU、伺服器、資料中心及電力基礎建設，龐大資金需求使融資能力逐漸成為決定企
業成長速度的重要因素。相較具備穩定營業現金流與龐大資產規模的大型科技平台，
新興 AI 雲端服務商如 CoreWeave 及 Nebius 高度依賴外部融資，因而更容易受到借
貸成本、信用利差及投資人風險偏好變化影響。我們認為，這次修正並不代表 AI 運算

需求突然消失，而是市場開始重新評估不同企業能否負擔下一階段的擴張成本。
4. 半導體設備財報強勁卻遭賣壓，市場提高獲利兌現標準：Lam Research 於 10 月 21
日公布截至 2026 年 9 月季度（FY2027 Q1）營收 82.19 億美元，年增 54.4%、季增
22%，非 GAAP 營業利益率達 40.2%，下一季營收指引為 86 億美元、上下浮動 4 億
美元。然而，即使營運仍快速成長，10 月 22 日股價仍大幅下跌。我們研判，市場已
由過去重視 AI 訂單與營收成長，轉向同時檢視未來成長能否超越原先偏高預期，以及
AI 投資是否具備足夠的財務報酬。當股價已反映高度成長期待，即使財報表現良好，
也可能因評價調整與籌碼降溫而承壓。
5. 油價再度上漲，Fed 政策風險仍可能限制科技股評價：儘管 10 月 22 日美債殖利率因
避險需求下滑，但能源價格上漲仍可能延緩通膨降溫，使 Fed 後續政策路徑維持不確
定性。若油價持續高檔，不僅可能推升企業運輸、電力及營運成本，也將增加通膨預

期，進而限制長天期美債殖利率的下行空間。因此，科技股短線同時面臨 AI 投資報酬
受到重新檢視，以及能源價格與利率政策不確定的雙重壓力。後續 10 月 28 日 FOMC
會後聲明及主席記者會，將是評估利率環境的重要依據。
三、 市場看法：
1. 股市下跌但多數個股上漲，顯示賣壓集中於大型科技股：10 月 22 日 S&P 500 指數下
跌 1.21%，但根據美聯社（AP）報導，約三分之二成分股逆勢上漲，追蹤 S&P 500
等權重指數的 ETF（RSP）更上漲 0.73%，道瓊工業指數亦小幅收紅，呈現市值加權
指數與市場廣度明顯背離的現象。這反映本波修正主要集中於權重較高的 AI 及大型科
技股，而非整體美股同步走弱。相較上週以防禦性類股輪動為主的情況，本週更值得
注意的是市場對高資本支出及高評價科技企業的重新定價，資金仍持續流向其他產業
，而非全面性風險撤離。只要賣壓未進一步擴散，現階段仍較接近科技股評價調整與
類股輪動，而非整體美股多頭格局反轉。
2. 台積電財測再度上修，顯示 AI 需求尚未出現結構性反轉：台積電 10 月 15 日法說會

將 2026 年美元營收成長預估由原先略高於 40%上修至約 44%，第三季營收達新台幣
1.49 兆元、年增 50.9%，稅後純益年增 68.7%，每股盈餘 29.43 元、同樣年增 68.7%
；第四季美元營收預估為 523～543 億美元，以中間值計算季增約 14%。此外，Lam
Research 截至 9 月季度營收年增 54.4%，亦顯示半導體設備需求仍強勁。前述這些

確性不做任何擔保，如有錯漏或疏忽，本行及本行之任何董事或受僱人，毋須負任何法律責任。任何人因信賴此等資料而做出或改變投資決
策，應審慎衡量本身之需求及投資風險，自行承擔一切投資風險並自負盈虧，本行不負任何法律責任。本報告受中華民國著作權法保護為元
大銀行所有，非經本行同意，不得將本報告內容之一部或全部加以複製、轉載或散布。本報告所載述的意見本行可隨時予以更改或撤回，恕
不另行通知。
數據與近期科技股重挫形成明顯對比，反映目前市場主要擔憂 AI 投資回收及股票評價
，而非半導體終端需求同步惡化。只要大型雲端服務商未全面縮減 AI 資本支出，先進
製程與設備需求中期仍具支撐。
3. AI 投資進入報酬驗證階段，營收成長能否追上資本投入成為關鍵：我們認為，AI 產業
目前正由過去的資本支出擴張階段，逐步轉向投資報酬率與自由現金流的驗證階段。
以 Meta 為例，第三季營收仍成長 26%，但資本支出已超過營業現金流，顯示即使
AI 技術有助於改善廣告推薦及營收變現，龐大基礎建設投入仍可能使短期自由現金流

承壓。不過，資料中心及運算設備通常具有多年使用年限，不能僅以單季現金流評估
整體投資成敗。真正關鍵在於未來 AI 服務收入、營業利益及現金流成長，能否逐步抵
銷前期資本投入。具備明確商業化模式、較高資本效率及穩定現金流的企業，後續表
現可望優於高度依賴融資與成長預期的業者。
4. AI 融資風險仍集中於部分企業，尚不足以認定信用危機全面擴散：Meta Q3 財報顯示
，該公司持有 824.5 億美元現金、約當現金及有價證券，顯示其具備一定財務緩衝能
力；相較之下，新興 AI 雲端業者因資料中心建置成本龐大、現金流尚未成熟，對信用
市場與外部融資條件的敏感度相對較高。雖然 AI 資本支出引發投資人重新評估企業信
用風險，但我們認為目前仍缺乏足夠證據認定風險已擴散至整體投資級債市場或銀行
體系。然而，後續仍宜密切觀察 AI 相關企業 CDS 與公司債利差變化、再融資能力及
主要金融機構授信條件對於科技類股及整體股債市場的後續影響。
5. 股價修正不代表企業獲利同步下修，財報表現仍有機會支撐科技股：儘管近期半導體

及 AI 相關股價回落，但台積電與 Lam Research 最新財報顯示，部分供應鏈企業營收
及獲利仍維持高度成長，反映股票價格與企業基本面短期可能出現明顯落差。當市場
因融資及資本支出疑慮而降低評價倍數時，即使企業獲利持續增加，股價也可能暫時
承壓。然而，若後續大型科技公司公布的雲端營收、AI 訂單與獲利展望持續優於預期
，企業 EPS 上修仍有機會抵銷部分本益比壓縮壓力。因此，目前尚不宜將科技股價格
修正直接等同於 AI 產業景氣反轉，企業獲利能否持續兌現，仍是中期市場方向的重要
支撐。

策，應審慎衡量本身之需求及投資風險，自行承擔一切投資風險並自負盈虧，本行不負任何法律責任。本報告受中華民國著作權法保護為元
大銀行所有，非經本行同意，不得將本報告內容之一部或全部加以複製、轉載或散布。本報告所載述的意見本行可隨時予以更改或撤回，恕
不另行通知。
6. Fed 與大型科技股財報進入驗證期，短線震盪可能延續：Fed 將於 10 月 27～28 日舉
行利率決策會議，市場除關注是否調整政策利率外，更應重視會後聲明及主席記者會
對通膨、油價與後續利率路徑的態度。另一方面，大型雲端服務商後續公布的財報，
將提供 AI 資本支出、雲端營收及投資效率的最新證據。短線科技股仍可能受到市場評
價偏高、融資風險及地緣政治因素影響而維持震盪；不過，只要企業獲利成長與 AI
需求未出現結構性反轉，中期科技產業基本面仍具支撐，現階段維持短線審慎、中期
偏正向的看法。
四、市場展望：

1. 科技股短線宜審慎觀察，中期仍看好具基本面支撐的 AI 產業：近期科技股受到 AI 資本支
出、融資能力與評價調整等因素影響，短線波動可能延續，尤其高度依賴外部資金及獲利
尚未成熟的 AI 基礎建設業者，仍面臨較大不確定性。然而，台積電及 Lam Research 最
新財報顯示，先進製程與半導體設備需求尚未出現全面轉弱。我們認為，科技股中期展望
仍偏正向，但市場將更重視企業營收成長、訂單能見度、自由現金流及資本效率。相較缺
乏穩定獲利的 AI 概念企業，具技術優勢、財務結構穩健及獲利成長能見度的科技企業，
風險報酬相對有利。
2. AI 產業由全面成長轉向基本面分化，現金流將成為選股關鍵：隨著大型科技企業持續擴
大 AI 基礎建設投資，市場對未來投資報酬率的要求將同步提高。就目前已公布的科技股
財報結果來看，AI 產業長期成長趨勢尚未改變，但後續股價表現將更加分化，具備穩定
現金流、明確訂單及商業化能力的企業，相對有利；反之，對於高度依賴外部融資、自由
現金流持續為負且缺乏明確獲利時程的企業，仍宜維持審慎。

3. 美債避險功能重現，中天期債券風險報酬相對均衡：半導體類股 10/22 重挫，美國公債
價格上揚、10 年期殖利率盤中跌破 5%，顯示股市風險偏好下降時，美債仍可提供一定
避險效果。雖然 Fed 政策路徑及能源價格仍存在不確定性，但預期相較受政策利率影響
較大的短天期公債，以及受財政赤字、國債供給與期限溢價影響的超長天期公債，中天期
美國公債的風險報酬仍相對較為均衡。投資級債因企業信用基本面整體仍具韌性，中期維
持偏正向看法；高收益債則須留意 AI 相關發行人信用風險上升及利差擴大的影響。

2026年 11月 16日 全7頁

Indicators Update 
2026年 7-9月期 GDP（1次速報） 
個人消費の弱さと外需の下押しで、4四半期ぶりのマイナス成長に

経済調査部 チーフエコノミスト 神田 慶司 
  エコノミスト 畑中 宏仁

[要約] 
◼ 2026年 7-9月期の実質 GDP成長率は前期比年率▲0.1％（前期比▲0.0％）と小幅なが
らも 4 四半期ぶりのマイナス成長となった。設備投資が増加した一方、個人消費が 2
四半期連続で減少し、輸出減と輸入増から外需も成長率を押し下げた。在庫変動を除
く実質最終需要は前期比年率▲2.1％と低迷するなど、内容は悪い。 
◼ 10-12月期の実質 GDP成長率は前期比年率＋0％台前半と小幅ながらもプラス成長に転
じると見込んでいる。個人消費は小幅に増加し、設備投資も増加するだろう。輸出は
米国向けを中心に増加するものの、輸入の伸びが上回り、外需の寄与度は 2四半期連
続のマイナスになるとみている。リスク要因は AI関連需要の下振れと中東情勢の悪化
であり、輸出や個人消費などへの影響に注意が必要だ。

※当社は、11月 20日（金）に「第231回日本経済予測」の発表を予定している。

実質GDPは小幅ながらも 4四半期ぶりのマイナス成長 
実質GDP成長率は前期比年率▲0.1％と市場予想を下回り、内容も悪い 
2026年 7-9月期の実質 GDP成長率は、1次速報値で前期比年率▲0.1％（前期比▲0.0％）だ
った。小幅ながらも 4四半期ぶりのマイナス成長となり、市場予想（QUICK調査中央値、前期
比年率＋0.2％）を下回った。なお、12月 8日に公表される 2次速報では 2025年度国民経済計
算年次推計の反映により、幅広い需要項目が改定される可能性がある。 
設備投資が増加に転じ、実質雇用者報酬の増加が続くなど緩やかな景気の拡大基調は続いて
いるとみられるが、在庫変動を除いた実質最終需要は前期比年率▲2.1％であり、個人消費が 2
四半期連続で減少するなど内容は悪い。 
また、交易利得・損失は7-9月期で▲11.4兆円（年率換算額）で1、実質GDPに交易利得・損
失を加えた実質 GDI（国内総所得）成長率は前期比年率▲1.6％だった。原油高などによる交易
条件の悪化で所得が海外に流出し、交易損失は前期から 2.2兆円拡大した。国内の所得環境は
GDPで見る以上に悪化したが、政府のエネルギー高対策が家計や企業への影響を一定程度緩和
した。 
実質 GDPを需要項目別に見ると（図表 2）、民需関連では個人消費・住宅投資が減少した一方、
設備投資は増加した。公需関連では政府消費と公共投資が減少した。公的在庫変動については、
7-9月期に大規模な石油の国家備蓄放出が行われなかったため、実質 GDP成長率への寄与度は
前期比＋0.5％pt と成長率を大きく押し上げた。外需関連では輸出減と輸入増により、純輸出
は実質GDP成長率を押し下げた。 
図表2：実質 GDP成長率と需要項目別の寄与度

（出所）内閣府統計より大和総研作成

1 交易条件（＝輸出デフレーター/輸入デフレーター）の変化による実質所得の増減で、プラスの場合は「交易 
利得」、マイナスの場合は「交易損失」。公表値は 2020暦年連鎖価格のデフレーターから算出されているた 
め（基準年の交易利得・損失額はゼロ）、交易利得・損失額はあくまでも基準年からの交易条件の変化の影響 
である点に留意が必要。

### Page 3

1

3 / 7

GDPデフレーターは前年同期比＋2.4％と 19四半期連続のプラスだった。また、単位労働コ
スト（＝名目雇用者報酬÷実質 GDP）は同＋4.4％と 14四半期連続のプラスで、賃金面からの
物価上昇圧力は足元でも継続している（巻末の関連指標①下段左）。

個人消費は、4-6月期が下方修正された上で 2四半期連続の減少 
個人消費は前期比▲0.3％と 2四半期連続で減少した。公表前の 4-6月期の個人消費は同＋
0.0％だったが、インバウンド消費額が上方修正されたことで同▲0.3％へと引き下げられた2。
7-9月期は一部耐久財の反動減や、9 月の天候不順といった一時的な下押し要因があったもの
の、所得環境が改善する中で 4-6月期から 2四半期連続で減少したところに、個人消費の弱さ
が見られる。 
国内家計最終消費支出を財・サービス別に見ると、耐久財（前期比▲3.5％）や半耐久財
（同▲1.4％）、非耐久財（同▲0.2％）がいずれも減少した一方、サービス（同＋0.1％）は増
加した（巻末の関連指標①上段左）。内閣府によると、耐久財ではエアコンなどが、半耐久財
では衣服などが、非耐久財ではその他個人ケア用器具（歯ブラシなど）などが押し下げたとい
う。4-6月期には、自動車税の環境性能割廃止や、2027年 4月に予定されているエアコンの省
エネ基準引き上げを見越した買い替え需要が耐久財消費を後押ししたが、その効果がはく落し
た。衣料品などの不振は天候不順が影響したとみられる。一方、サービスでは教育や携帯電話
通信料などが押し上げたという。 
実質雇用者報酬（家計最終消費支出デフレーターで実質化）は前期比＋0.3％と6四半期連続
で増加した（巻末の関連指標①上段右）。労働力調査（総務省）に見る雇用者数は同 0.0％で、
雇用者数で除した1人あたり実質雇用者報酬は同＋0.3％と6四半期連続で増加した（前年同期
比では3四半期連続のプラス）。

設備投資は3四半期ぶりの増加、住宅投資は2四半期連続の減少 
設備投資は前期比＋1.6％と 3四半期ぶりに増加した。建設投資は減少したものの、4-6月期
に特殊要因で減少していた研究開発投資で反動増が表れたほか、機械投資が増加したようだ。
内閣府によると、機械投資はサーバーや半導体製造装置などが増加に寄与したという。 
住宅投資は前期比▲1.1％と2四半期連続で減少した。原材料費高騰による建設資材価格の上
昇や人件費の上昇が重しとなっており、住宅着工戸数は 6月にかけて増加していたものの、7
月以降は下落に転じている（巻末の関連指標②上段右）。

2 家計最終消費支出は国内消費支出から非居住者家計の国内での直接購入（インバウンド消費）を控除して算
出されているため、インバウンド消費の上方修正は家計最終消費支出の下方修正につながる。

### Page 4

1

4 / 7

公共投資は 2四半期連続の減少、政府消費は6四半期ぶりに減少 
公共投資は前期比▲1.1％と2四半期連続で減少した。資材価格の高騰や人手不足などが重し
になっている上、2025年度補正予算に盛り込まれた公共事業の執行が一巡したとみられる。 
政府消費は前期比▲0.0％と小幅ながらも6四半期ぶりに減少した。内閣府によると、医療・
介護費が増加したものの、中間消費を中心にデフレーターが上昇したことが実質成長率の押し
下げにつながったという。 
また、実質 GDP成長率に対する公的在庫変動の寄与度は、7-9月期は大規模な石油の国家備
蓄放出が行われなかったため前期比＋0.5％ptとなり、成長率を大幅に押し上げた。後述のよ
うに原油の代替調達の進展によって、輸入の増加が実質 GDP成長率を押し下げたが、公的在庫
変動の増加がおおむね相殺した形だ。

輸出減と輸入増により、外需寄与度は前期比▲0.6％pt 
輸出は前期比▲0.2％と4四半期ぶりに減少した。財は増加したが、サービスが減少した。内
閣府によると、財では半導体製造装置や石油製品、基礎化学製品などが増加したという。サー
ビスでは、4-6月期に特殊要因で大幅に増加していた研究開発サービスに加え、インバウンド
や産業財産権等使用料なども減少したようだ。 
輸入は前期比＋2.5％と 2 四半期ぶりに増加した。サービスは減少したものの、財が増加し
た。財では、中東情勢の影響により 4-6月期に減少していた石油・天然ガスが代替調達の進展
によって増加したほか、非鉄金属やサーバー等も増加したようだ。サービスでは、アウトバウ
ンドは増加したものの、内閣府によると著作権等使用料や研究開発サービス、専門・経営コン
サルティングサービスなどが減少したという。 
輸出の減少と輸入の増加により、純輸出（外需）の実質 GDP成長率に対する寄与度は前期比
▲0.6％ptと、4四半期ぶりのマイナスとなった。

10-12月期の実質 GDPは内需と輸出に支えられ、小幅のプラス成長か 
2026年 10-12月期の実質 GDP成長率は前期比年率＋0％台前半と、小幅ながらもプラス成長
に転じると見込んでいる。 
個人消費は小幅な増加に転じるとみている。現金給与総額は前年同月比で 3％を超える伸び
が続いており、10月以降は各地で最低賃金が引き上げられていることから名目賃金の高い伸び
は続くだろう。一方、食料品等を中心に物価上昇率は高まっている。エネルギー価格について
も、資源価格高騰がラグを持って反映されることに加え、補助の一時終了や縮小も物価の押し
上げ要因となり、10-12月期の実質賃金上昇率を押し下げるだろう。設備投資は建設資材価格
と人件費の上昇が建設投資を下押しするものの、省力化投資やAI関連投資を背景に増加基調が

### Page 5

1

5 / 7

続くと見込む。 
輸出は増加に転じるとみている。米国の設備投資はAI関連需要を背景に増加が続くだろう。
米国内での設備投資増加は米国向け輸出の追い風となり、財輸出は増加すると見込んでいる。
一方、インバウンドは中国政府による日本への渡航自粛要請が続く中、増加は緩やかなものと
なるだろう。 
輸入は増加するとみている。原油輸入の回復が続くことに加え、AI関連需要を背景に電算機
類等の輸入も増加するだろう。輸入の増加が輸出の増加を上回ることで、外需の寄与度は 2四
半期連続のマイナスになると見込んでいる。 
10-12月期以降を含めたリスク要因は、AI関連需要の下振れと中東情勢の悪化である。足元
の米国経済では設備投資が景気を下支えしており、とりわけデータセンターや半導体などの IT
関連投資がけん引役となっている。AI関連サービスの世界的な利用拡大に伴い、計算能力の増
強に向けた大型投資が今後も続く可能性は高い。一方で、一部のAI関連企業では収益性や財務
面への懸念が広がっているほか、米中間選挙でAIデータセンター建設の一時停止や規制強化な
どを訴えた民主党急進左派候補者が当選するなど、AI投資を取り巻く環境には変化の兆しも見
られる。ハイパースケーラーが設備投資の拡大ペースを落とせば、半導体や情報通信機器など
の需要を下押しし、関連産業の生産減や輸出減などを通じて実体経済にも波及するだろう3。ま
た、中東情勢が悪化し、原油価格がさらに高騰すれば、物価高を通じて個人消費を一段と下押
しするだろう。 
2027年度までの経済見通しの詳細などについては、11月 20日に発表予定の「第 231回日本
経済予測」を参照されたい。

3 詳細は、畑中宏仁・神田慶司「ハイパースケーラーの投資動向が世界経済に与える影響」（大和総研レポー
ト、2026年11月 12日）を参照。

### Page 1

Better Interest Spread — Group interest spread widened 8bps qoq to 6.95%, 
mainly driven by better China spread. China spread improved 19bps qoq, as loan 
yield increased 8bps qoq and funding cost was 11bps lower qoq. Taiwan loan spread 
narrowed 7bps qoq, primarily due to 6bps qoq higher funding cost amid Fed rate 
hike and tightening domestic liquidity. 
Muted Loan Growth — Group loans grew by 1% qoq/4% yoy, mainly driven by 
ASEAN portfolio growth and RMB appreciation. Taiwan portfolio was flat qoq/-1% 
yoy. China portfolio was +2% qoq/flat yoy in local currency (+3% qoq/+11% yoy in 
NTD). Management suggested that the China business has achieved positive loan 
growth in the past three months and they expected positive momentum ahead. 
ASEAN portfolio was+2% qoq/+10% yoy,  
Broadly Stable China Asset Quality, Higher ASEAN Credit Cost — Group 
delinquency ratio slightly increased 3bps qoq to 5.16%. Taiwan delinquency ratio 
increased 20bps qoq, while NPL formation improved qoq. China delinquency ratio 
was stable qoq, but NPL formation slightly picked up qoq per our estimates. Group 
credit cost was 2.49%, +11bps qoq, mainly due to higher ASEAN credit costs on the 
back of macro uncertainties especially in Cambodia. 
Implications — Despite steady profit growth in 10M26 and stabilizing China asset 
quality, Chailease shares continued to de-rate this year amid concerns over growth. 
Management sounded more upbeat on China loan growth outlook at the results 
briefing, and the impact from auto financing business on Taiwan loan growth should 
gradually fade. FY26E dividend yield of 6% is attractive, in our view.

### Page 2

Chailease Holding (5871.TW)
12 November 2026 Citi Research
2
Chailease Holding
Valuation
Our target price for Chailease of NT$130 is based on 1.2x 2026E P/B, using a Gordon Growth model. Our valuation
methodology takes into account a sustainable ROE estimate of 12.1%, a cost-of-equity assumption of 11.1%, and a long-term
growth rate of 6.0%. We use a blended approach to calculate both risk-free rates and long-term growth rates taking into
account local estimates on these parameters in the major markets in which Chailease operates, namely Taiwan, China, and

Thailand, and weighted them by asset mix.
Risks
Key downside risks that could impede the Chailease stock from reaching our target price are: [1] slower-than-expected asset
growth momentum; [2] asset quality deterioration in different markets; [3] higher operating expenses, which drag bottom-line
growth; [4] earlier-than-expected capital-raising that dilutes ROE and BVPS; and [5] ASEAN expansion that is slow in
contributing to group earnings. Key upsides risks to our target price include: [1] faster-than-expected asset quality recovery as

macro uncertainties ease; [2] higher-than-expected asset growth momentum.

Guide to Citi Research Fundamental Research Investment Ratings:
Citi Research stock recommendations include an investment rating and an optional risk rating to highlight high risk stocks.

Risk rating takes into account both price volatility and fundamental criteria. Stocks will either have no risk rating or a High risk
rating assigned.
Investment Ratings: Citi Research investment ratings are Buy, Neutral and Sell. Our ratings are a function of analyst
expectations of expected total return ("ETR") and risk. ETR is the sum of the forecast price appreciation (or depreciation) plus
the dividend yield for a stock within the next 12 months.  The target price is based on a 12 month time horizon. The Investment

rating definitions are: Buy (1) ETR of 15% or more or 25% or more for High risk stocks; and Sell (3) for negative ETR. Any covered
stock not assigned a Buy or a Sell is a Neutral (2). For stocks rated Neutral (2), if an analyst believes that there are insufficient
valuation drivers and/or investment catalysts to derive a positive or negative investment view, they may elect with the approval
of Citi Research management not to assign a target price and, thus, not derive an ETR. Citi Research may suspend its rating

and target price and assign “Rating Suspended” status for regulatory and/or internal policy reasons. Citi Research may also
suspend its rating and target price and assign "Under Review" status for other exceptional circumstances (e.g. lack of
information critical to the analyst's thesis, trading suspension) affecting the company and/or trading in the company's
securities. In both such situations, the rating and target price will show as “—“ and “-“ respectively in the rating history price

chart. Prior to 11 April 2022 Citi Research assigned “Under Review” status to both situations and prior to 11 Nov 2020 only in
exceptional circumstances. As soon as practically possible, the analyst will publish a note re-establishing a rating and
investment thesis. Investment ratings are determined by the ranges described above at the time of initiation of coverage, a
change in investment and/or risk rating, or a change in target price (subject to limited management discretion). At times, the

expected total returns may fall outside of these ranges because of market price movements and/or other short-term volatility
or trading patterns. Such interim deviations will be permitted but will become subject to review by Research Management.
Your decision to buy or sell a security should be based upon your personal investment objectives and should be made only
after evaluating the stock's expected performance and risk.
Catalyst Watch/Short Term Views (“STV”) Ratings Disclosure:

Catalyst Watch and STV Upside/Downside calls: Citi Research may also include a Catalyst Watch or STV Upside or Downside
call to indicate the analyst expects the share price to rise (fall) in absolute terms over a specified period of 30 or 90 days in
reaction to one or more specific near-term catalysts or events impacting the company or the market. A Catalyst Watch will be
published when Analyst confidence is high that an impact to share price will occur; it will be a STV when confidence level is

moderate. A Catalyst Watch or STV Upside/Downside call will automatically expire at the end of the specified 30/90 day
period. The Catalyst Watch will also be automatically removed if share price performance (calculated at market close) exceeds
15% against the direction of the call (unless over-ridden by the analyst). The analyst may also remove a Catalyst Watch or STV
call prior to the end of the specified period in a published research note. A Catalyst Watch/STV Upside or Downside call may be

buy recommendation and a Catalyst Watch/STV Downside call corresponds to a sell recommendation. Any stock not assigned
to a Catalyst Watch Upside, Catalyst Watch Downside, STV Upside, or STV Downside call is considered Catalyst Watch/STV

2026.11.17 
吳昆潮 C00269@ibfs.com.tw 
7788.TT 松川精密 
 趨勢不變 評價調升

[財務資訊更新，調升評價]   
   公司簡介 松川精密成立於 1974 年，主要從事繼電器之研發、製造及銷售，繼電器屬於自動控 制開關的一種，係以較小電流(訊號)連動大電流(電源)的元件。 
投資評等/目標價 重點摘要 
強力買進 348 
【前次投資建議】買進，348 
【大盤指數/股價】41040 / 178.5

➢ 高價庫存原料侵蝕毛利，3Q26 EPS 2.83 元  
松川精密 3Q26 營收 27.35 億元，QoQ+21.04%，YoY+78.84%，毛利
率 27.97%，稅後淨利 2.26 億元，QoQ-14.23%，YoY+103.23%，EPS 
2.83 元，低於投研部預期，主要歸因於毛利率季減 2.98 個百分點，包含
兩大因素：1)產品組合變化：完整認列台興營收，單季營收佔比 3.6%，
且高毛利儲能、AI 繼電器相關產品佔比下滑 1 至 2 個百分點；2)高價原
料成本影響：上半年白銀曾衝上每盎司 121.6 美元的歷史天價，公司 8~9
月出貨產品採用高價白銀原料。 
➢ 最壞情況已過，4Q26 獲利重返單季新高 
10 月營收 8.49 億元，MoM-3.19%，YoY+53.62%，達成率 32%，符
合預期，部分產品出貨受船期影響，致營收遞延認列，整體單季營收與
原先預估差異不大。毛利率方面，下半年白銀每季平均跌幅 12%~14%，
目前回落至 50 美元以下，原料平均成本已下降，10 月高價原料衝擊影
響性降低，毛利率回升，預期單季最壞情況已過，4Q26 毛利率回升至
29.11%，EPS 3.33 元，獲利重返成長。 
➢ AI 伺服器高壓直流架構，繼電器/接觸器產值增加近兩倍 
AI 伺服器從 54V 升級至±400V 或 800V 高壓直流架構，必須額外配置
高壓直流接觸器，技術門檻遠高於傳統產品，單價達到交流繼電器的 20
倍至 30 倍，投研部推測每機櫃至少需配置 12 至 28 顆不等，單機櫃產
值成長 198%。松川 225A/900V 產品已率先邁入小量出貨階段，直流接
觸器將是松川 2027 年的核心成長引擎，預估松川 2027 年 AI 伺服器繼
電器/接觸器營收成長 50%~60%，營收佔比 20.2%。 
➢ 評等調升至強力買進，目標價 348 元 
投研部預估松川精密 2026 年/2027 年 EPS 分別為 11.68 元及 15.58
元，2027 年各應用面展望樂觀，儲能及 AI 伺服器仍是主軸，雖然毛利
率較原先下修 1 個百點，但上修直流接觸器產值預估，加上費用控管優
於預期，2027 年整體獲利小幅上修。評價方面，目前松川本益比僅 11
倍，處於歷史 PE 區間 10X~30X 下緣，投資價值浮現，投研部將松川投
資評等，由買進調升至強力買進，目標價 348 元(2027F PE23X)。

高價庫存原料侵蝕毛利，3Q26 EPS 2.83 元，低於預期 
松川精密 3Q26 營收 27.35 億元，QoQ+21.04%，YoY+78.84%，毛利率 27.97%，
稅後淨利 2.26 億元，QoQ-14.23%，YoY+103.23%，EPS 2.83 元，低於投研部預期，
主要歸因於毛利率季減 2.98 個百分點，包含兩大因素：1)產品組合變化：完整認列台
興營收，單季營收佔比 3.6%，且高毛利儲能、AI 繼電器相關產品佔比下滑 1 至 2 個
百分點；2)高價原料成本衝擊：白銀在 2026 上半年曾衝上每盎司 121.6 美元的歷史
天價，公司 8~9 月出貨產品採用高價白銀原料，對 8~9 月的單月毛利率造成短期壓抑。

大。毛利率方面，下半年白銀每季平均跌幅 12%~14%，目前回落至 50 美元以下，原
料平均成本已下降，10 月高價原料衝擊影響性已降低，毛利率回升，預期單季最壞情
況已過，4Q26 毛利率回升至 29.11%，預估稅後淨利 2.65 億元，QoQ+17.58%，
YoY+139.24%，EPS 3.33 元。

### Page 3

- 3 -

個股報告 
民主黨勝選有利綠能產業發展，儲能剛性需求強 
根據產業統計，2026 年前三季全球儲能總併網量估計達 220 至 240 GWh，系統出貨
量約 420 GWh。受惠於第四季併網旺季，BloombergNEF 於 10 月將 2026 全年新增
裝機預估上調至 188 GW / 535 GWh，並預期 2027 年將進一步成長至 209 GW / 638 
GWh，容量年增 19.3%。太陽能方面，全球新增裝機在 2025 年創高後，2026 年受
中國電網消納限制影響，預計微幅回落至 584 至 638 GW；但海外市場逆勢成長 15%
至 313 GW。展望 2027 年，全球裝機預估將回升至 618 GW，產業呈現「光弱儲強」
趨勢，加速光儲一體化建置。 
綜合上述，全球光儲兩大核心市場皆集中於中美兩國，加上美國民主黨於 2026 年期
中選舉勝出並奪下國會主導權，綠能與清潔能源政策延續性獲得有力支撐，可望進一
步推升當地光儲建置動能。中美兩地領導廠商均為松川精密的核心客戶，受惠於美系
客戶新一代儲能櫃容量由 3.9 MWh 提升至 5 MWh，工作電流增加帶動接觸器規格與
單價雙雙看漲，預估 2027 年松川在儲能、太陽能及微電網業務將維持 10%~15%的
成長。 
AI 伺服器高壓直流架構，繼電器/接觸器產值增加近兩倍 
AI 伺服器從 54V 升級至±400V 或 800V 高壓直流架構，機櫃配電單元(PDU)面臨嚴
峻的高壓電弧安全挑戰，必須額外配置高壓直流接觸器(DC Contactor)，為在毫秒內
強制切斷(滅弧)800V 高壓直流電弧，產品內部高度仰賴磁吹滅弧設計或真空密封充氣
(氫氣／氮氣)工藝，技術門檻遠高於傳統交流繼電器(AC Relay)，單價達到交流繼電器
的 20 倍至 30 倍。在用量方面，每座機櫃 PDU 至少需配置 12 顆，主流高階規格更
需 24 顆至 28 顆，單機櫃直流接觸器產值為既有交流繼電器的 2 至 3 倍。松川
225A/900V 產品已率先邁入小量出貨階段，未來隨著高壓直流架構全面放量，直流接
觸器將正式擔綱松川 2027 年的核心成長引擎。在額外增加直流接觸器需求下，投研
部推測高壓直流架構(不含自動轉換開關 ATS)，AI 伺服器繼電器/接觸器單機櫃產值將
從 7,000 元增加至 20,900 元，YoY+198%，預估松川 2027 年 AI 伺服器繼電器/接觸
器營收成長 50%~60%，營收佔比 20.2%。 
預估松川 2027 年 EPS 15.58 元，評等調升至強力買進，目標價 348 元 
預估松川精密 2026 年營收 95.52 億元，YoY+48.49%，毛利率 28.80%，稅後淨利
9.31 億元，YoY+246.03%，EPS 11.68 元；預估 2027 年 113.14 億元，YoY+18.45%，
毛利率 30.53%，稅後淨利 12.43 億元，YoY+33.42%，EPS 15.58 元。2027 年各應
用面展望樂觀，儲能及 AI 伺服器仍是主軸，雖然毛利率較原先下修 1 個百點，但上修
直流接觸器產值預估，加上費用控管優於預期，2027 年整體獲利小幅上修。評價方
面，目前松川本益比僅 11 倍，處於歷史 PE 區間 10X~30X 下緣，投資價值浮現，投
研部將松川投資評等，由買進調升至強力買進，目標價 348 元(2027F PE23X)。

자료: 유안타증권

Company Report  2026.11.12

상선, 방산, 해양, 엔진 모든 사업 영위하는 전천후 기업 
경쟁사 대비 1) 가장 넓은 사업 영역을 보유, 2) 시가총액 또한 높음. 이러한 특성은 동사의 
사업 포트폴리오 균형 관점에서 장점이나, 반대로 투자 매력의 집중을 일정 부분 희석. 경쟁
사 대비 주가 상승 트리거가 가장 폭넓게 존재하나, 상승 탄력은 그만큼 제한. 
상선: FY27F 매출인식은 FY23-25 수주 물량, 가스선 매출비중 하락 
FY27F 상선부문 매출은 FY23-25 수주 물량이 인식되며 (일부 FY26F 수주), FY25 수주
한 물량이 연간 40% 수준의 가장 높은 매출기여도를 보일 것. 2H27F부터 올해 수주한 일
부 선박의 매출인식 또한 발생하며, 연중 가스선 매출 비중이 하락하고 컨테이너선 매출 비
중이 상승. 
방산: 좋게 보자면 불안하고, 나쁘게 보자면 아쉬운 
MASGA를 국내 야드에서 진행한다는 대전제로 경쟁사와 같은 적정가치를 부여해 왔으나, 
미국 현지 건조와 같은 여러 가능성을 고려하면 다음과 같은 불확실성 존재: 1) 중간지주사
와의 미국내 JV 설립 관련 투자 지분 미정, 2) 신규 조선소 건립시 미국내 수주와 매출까지 
걸리는 시간. 
투자의견 BUY 유지, 다 깎아서 봐도 충분한 상승여력 
상선부문 26.5조, 방산 8조, 선박엔진 9조, 발전엔진 (FY30F 기준) 13.5조 수준의 SOTP 
밸류에이션 기반하여 목표주가 643,000원 제시. 발전엔진 증설로 인한 미래가치를 반영하
나, 상선부문 Target P/O multiple 하향 (0.7x, 업종 공통)과 MASGA 적정가치 하향으로 
목표주가 자체는 하락. 보수적 멀티플 제시함에도 주가 상승 여력은 충분히 확보된 상황. 업
종 전체적 긍정요인 외의 동사 주가 상승 트리거는 1) 미국 현지 조선소 투자주체 발탁, 2) 
DC향 발전엔진 추가 수주, 3) MASGA 국내 건조 물량 수주.

## Document 276: Anthropic上市首日散户成交额创纪录，但近半交易都在卖！

Source PDF: all report pdf/Anthropic上市首日散户成交额创纪录，但近半交易都在卖！.pdf
Report source: WallstreetCN (Global)
Date: 2026-11-20
Pages: 3
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

Anthropic 上市⾸⽇散⼾总成交额达 7.72 亿美元创历史新⾼，但近半数成交来⾃卖出，净买
⼊仅 6000 万美元，远逊于 2.5 亿美元的预期。受⼤盘下跌拖累、⾼⽐例散⼾获利了结及加密
永续合约提前分流影响，该股⾼开低⾛，后续仍⾯临巨额低价散⼾筹码的潜在抛压。
摘要：
Anthropic 周四登陆纳斯达克，散⼾交易额打破历史纪录，却难掩内⾥的疲态 —— 近半数成交来
⾃卖出⽅向，净买⼊规模远低于 Vanda 此前的预估，显⽰这场史上最⼤ IPO 的散⼾热情远⽐表⾯
数字所呈现的更为脆弱。
据零售资⾦研究机构 Vanda Research 统计， Anthropic ⾸⽇散⼾总成交额达 7.72 亿美元，超越今
年 6 ⽉ SpaceX 上市⾸⽇的 6.69 亿美元，创下 Vanda ⾃ 2012 年有数据以来 IPO ⾸⽇散⼾交易量的最
⾼纪录。
然⽽，这⼀纪录背后暗藏隐忧：Vanda 分析师 Viraj Patel 指出，买⼊占⽐仅为 53.9% ，低于
SpaceX ⾸⽇的 58.8% ，折算后散⼾净买⼊仅约 6000 万美元，不及此前预测值 2.5 亿美元的四分

之⼀，与预期相差⾜⾜ 1.9 亿美元。
Anthropic 开盘于 111.50 美元，较 83 美元发⾏价⾼出 34% ，盘中短暂触及 115 美元附近，市值⼀
度突破 2 万亿美元。但此后股价持续下滑，收盘于 97.17 美元，较发⾏价仅上涨 17% ，较开盘价
则下跌约 13% 。与此同时，标普 500 指数当⽇下跌 2% ，录得连续第六个交易⽇下跌，道琼斯指
数收盘跌破 50000 点，整体市场环境对这场 IPO 构成了明显拖累。
创纪录成交量下的 " 卖出潮 "
Vanda 的数据揭⽰了散⼾⾏为模式上的结构性差异。在 7.72 亿美元的总成交中，买⼊⽅向约 4.16
亿美元，卖出⽅向约 3.56 亿美元，净买⼊仅 6000 万美元。横向对⽐， SpaceX ⾸⽇散⼾净买⼊约
1.18 亿美元， Coinbase 2021 年 4 ⽉上市⾸⽇约 9200 万美元， Anthropic 以 6000 万美元位居第
三，仅略⾼于 Uber 、 Arm 和 Snowflake 。
从盘中节奏来看，开盘后⾸个 30 分钟，散⼾净买⼊约 1900 万美元，尚属可观。但此后买盘迅速

消退，第⼆、第三个半⼩时内净买⼊仅各约 200 ⾄ 300 万美元，⽽ SpaceX 在同⼀时段仍维持每
个时间段 2000 万美元以上的净买⼊。按 10 分钟区间观察， Anthropic 开盘后⼤部分时间买⼊占⽐
维持在 50% ⾄ 54% 之间，接近多空均衡，仅在开盘约四⼩时后、股价已⼤幅低于开盘价时，才
出现⼀个超过 70% 买⼊占⽐的时间段 —— 彼时⼊场的，是逢低抄底者，⽽⾮追涨资⾦。
⾸⻚ 资讯 快讯 ⾏情 ⽇历 APP VIP 会员 ⼤师课 ⻅闻历 搜索

### Page 2

这⼀模式的形成，与本次 IPO 特殊的配售结构密切相关。据 CNBC 报道， Anthropic 最终向散⼾分
配了约 15% 的发⾏份额，对应约 1.47 亿股、发⾏价下合计约 120 亿美元，远超通常 5% ⾄ 10% 的
散⼾配售⽐例。在股价以 34% 的涨幅开盘后，相当⼀部分持有低价筹码的散⼾选择兑现收益，
⽽⾮继续持有⼀家以超过 18 倍年化收⼊估值定价的公司。
加密衍⽣品市场 " 抢跑 " 了 IPO
Vanda 提出的第⼀个解释，涉及加密货币衍⽣品平台对传统 IPO 市场的分流效应。据 Vanda 统
计， Anthropic 在 Hyperliquid 平台上的永续合约当⽇成交额达 17.5 亿美元，仅在现货开盘后的约
四⼩时内，该平台成交量约为 Vanda 统计的全部散⼾现货成交额的 1.5 倍。 SpaceX 上市当⽇该⽐
例为 1.2 倍。 Anthropic 永续合约未平仓量⼀度创下 4.5 亿美元的历史纪录，且⼤部分头⼨在正式
开盘前已经建⽴。
这意味着，有意布局 Anthropic 的边际投机者，在 IPO 前数⽇便已通过永续合约、甚⾄带杠杆地

完成了建仓。等到纳斯达克临近中午才正式开放交易时，这笔交易对许多⼈⽽⾔早已是 " 旧闻 " 。
Patel 在此前的预测中，正是通过 Hyperliquid 永续合约成交量来推算散⼾净买⼊预期值 2.5 亿美
元，⽽实际数字的⼤幅落空，部分源于预期需求已被衍⽣品市场提前消化。
宏观逆⻛是更根本的制约
Patel 倾向于将散⼾买盘不及预期，主要归因于更⼴泛的市场⻛险规避情绪。 Anthropic 是 Vanda
样本中唯⼀⼀只在标普 500 单⽇下跌 2% 的背景下上市的⼤型 IPO——SpaceX 上市当⽇，该指数
上涨 0.54% 。在其他持仓普遍飘绿的市场环境中，追逐热⻔新股要容易得多；⽽当⽇逾 93% 的标
普 500 成分股收跌，债券与美元⾛强，⻛险资产全⾯承压。
机构层⾯同样未能提供⽀撑。⾼盛 Prime 经纪台数据显⽰，基本⾯多空对冲基⾦净杠杆率降⾄
41.6% ，为五年低点，低于去年 4 ⽉时的⽔平；过去六周全球信息技术板块对冲基⾦卖出速度创
⼗年之最， z 分数达 -4.4 ，该板块多空⽐从 2026 年峰值 2.9 降⾄ 1.79 。此外，据估算，当⽇收盘前

杠杆 ETF 复合体需卖出约 220 ⾄ 230 亿美元以完成再平衡，系统性基⾦另需摆动约 40 亿美元⾄卖
出⽅向，以波动率控制策略为主导。买盘缺席与被动强制抛售的叠加，使市场在全天持续承
压。

### Page 3

⻛险提⽰及免责条款
市场有⻛险，投资需谨慎。本⽂不构成个⼈投资建议，也未考虑到个别⽤⼾特殊的投资⽬标、财务状况或需要。⽤⼾应考虑本⽂
中的任何意⻅、观点或结论是否符合其特定状况。据此投资，责任⾃负。
304 收藏 分享到 :
写评论
图⽚
散⼾转向指数产品， IPO 吸引⼒打折
在 Anthropic 之外， Vanda 数据显⽰，散⼾当⽇对个股整体呈净卖出状态，净卖出约 3200 万美
元，其中 AMD （当⽇跌逾 7% ）卖出最为集中。资⾦转⽽流向指数与杠杆产品：流⼊ SPY 约
9400 万美元，流⼊三倍做多半导体 ETF SOXL 约 9200 万美元。
这⼀数据揭⽰出两个耐⼈寻味的对⽐：其⼀，史上最热 IPO ⾸⽇吸引的散⼾净资⾦，不及⼀只普
通标普 500 指数基⾦或⼀只三倍杠杆半导体 ETF ；其⼆，就在上周，英伟达创始⼈ Jensen
Huang 辞职消息发布后⾸个 30 分钟内，散⼾净买⼊英伟达⾼达 1.11 亿美元，三⽇累计达 13 亿美
元 —— ⼀家深陷丑闻、 CEO 空缺、估值约 12 倍的英伟达，在半⼩时内吸引的散⼾资⾦，⼏乎是

Anthropic 全天的两倍。
后续⾛势：剩余筹码的压⼒悬⽽未决
Patel 并未断⾔散⼾买盘就此终结。他指出， SpaceX ⾸⽇仅占其⾸周散⼾净买⼊总量的 29% ，第
⼆⾄第五个交易⽇再累计贡献约 2.87 亿美元。若 Anthropic 遵循相同路径，⾸周合计或仍可达约 2
亿美元。
然⽽，两者所处的市场环境存在本质差异。 SpaceX 上市后股价⼀路⾛⾼，从 150 美元开盘攀升
⾄第三⽇的 225 美元历史⾼点，散⼾在绿⾊ K 线的驱动下持续加仓。 Anthropic ⾸⽇则以较开盘价
下跌 13% 收场，所处市场已连续六⽇下跌，次⽇⼜⾯临⽉度期权到期⽇。此外，经此前
Hyperliquid 市场消化与散⼾分配，约 120 亿美元的 83 美元低价筹码⽬前分散在各散⼾券商账⼾
中，当前持有者账⾯浮盈约 17% ，潜在卖出窗⼝随时间推移只会越来越宽。
在 SpaceX 案例中，上市后的核⼼问题是 " 散⼾还会再买多少 " ；在 Anthropic ，这个问题已悄然转
变为 " 散⼾还剩多少可以卖 " 。

## Document 310: CTCP Vàng Bạc Đá Quý Phú Nhuận - Cập nhật ĐHCĐ bất thường 21_10_2026 (2026-10-22)

Source PDF: all report pdf/CTCP Vàng Bạc Đá Quý Phú Nhuận -  Cập nhật ĐHCĐ bất thường 21_10_2026 (2026-10-22).pdf
Report source: KBSV (Company)
Date: 2026-10-22
Pages: 4
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

F I R S T  T O  M A R K E T

CTCP VÀNG BẠC ĐÁ QUÝ PHÚ NHUẬN (PNJ)  
Cập nhật ĐHĐCĐ bất thường 21/10/2026

Tái cơ cấu để xử lý áp lực thanh khoản  
— ĐHĐCĐ bất thường PNJ ngày 21/10/2026 đã thông qua điều chỉnh kế hoạch kinh 
doanh và phương án phát hành riêng lẻ để bổ sung vốn tái cấu trúc. Ban lãnh đạo đặt 
ra các vấn đề trọng yếu là đáp ứng nhu cầu thanh khoản và chuẩn bị nguồn lực để thực 
hiện các cam kết thu đổi đã tích lũy trong quá khứ. Bên cạnh đó, các cổ đông tham dự 
cũng đặt các câu hỏi liên quan đến tình hình hoạt động qua các điểm chính (1) dòng 
tiền hoạt động kinh doanh; (2) diễn biến thị trường sau khi PNJ áp dụng chính sách thu 
đổi mới; (3) hiệu quả hoạt động sau tái cấu trúc.

Nội dung chính tại đại hội 
— Theo Ban lãnh đạo, thông tin liên quan đến P-Lab từ đầu tháng 7 khiến khách hàng gia 
tăng bán lại sản phẩm. Tổng giá trị thu đổi và mua lại trong tháng đạt gần 6,000 tỷ VND, 
so với khoảng 7,000 tỷ VND trong cả năm 2025. Theo BCTC riêng, nguồn tiền khả dụng 
giảm từ khoảng 3,700 tỷ VND cuối Q2/2026 xuống 770 tỷ VND cuối Q3/2026, đã bao 
gồm khoản vay 700 tỷ VND từ Chủ tịch HĐQT. Tỷ trọng kim cương và trang sức kim 
cương trong tổng tồn kho tăng từ 41% lên 52%. 
— Q3/2026 ghi nhận lỗ sau thuế 258 tỷ VND trước phần tăng trích lập dự phòng. Để giảm 
áp lực thanh toán, PNJ kéo dài thời gian chi trả đối với kim cương lên 120 ngày từ tháng 
07/2026. Thay đổi chính sách cũng ảnh hưởng đến niềm tin khách hàng và sức mua, cụ 
thể doanh thu và lợi nhuận gộp Q3/2026 đạt lần lượt 6,995/609 tỷ VND, giảm 14%/62% 
YoY. Chi phí bán hàng và quản lý giảm 13% YoY chưa đủ bù đắp mức giảm lợi nhuận 
gộp, khiến PNJ lỗ sau thuế 258 tỷ VND, so với lãi 496 tỷ VND trong Q3/2025.

Biểu đồ 1. Kết quả kinh doanh theo quý  Biểu đồ 2. Diễn biến doanh thu thuần/ngày và doanh thu bán 
lẻ/ngày theo quý

### Page 2

2

F I R S T  T O  M A R K E T 
 — PNJ nâng giả định tỷ lệ thu đổi trên giá trị thu đổi còn lại lên 80%, so với 28.8% đối với 
nhóm sản phẩm giai đoạn 2020–2026 trong ước tính tại BCTC bán niên 2026. Tổng dự 
phòng tổn thất dự kiến tăng từ 2,267 tỷ VND lên 7,071 tỷ VND. Kế hoạch điều chỉnh ghi 
nhận doanh thu 39,057 tỷ VND, lỗ trước thuế 6,059 tỷ VND và lỗ sau thuế 6,271 tỷ VND. 
Theo Ban lãnh đạo, LNST loại trừ dự phòng ước khoảng 800 tỷ VND, chủ yếu nhờ kết 
quả Q1/2026. Khoản dự phòng phản ánh tổn thất ước tính cho toàn bộ kịch bản, không 
đồng nghĩa toàn bộ giá trị sẽ được chi trả ngay trong kỳ. 
— Giá trị cam kết thu đổi tích lũy đối với kim cương và trang sức kim cương tại cuối 
Q3/2026 được PNJ ước tính khoảng 21,000 tỷ VND. Với hàng đã bán, doanh nghiệp dự 
kiến chuyển đổi quyền lợi khách hàng sang sản phẩm và các chương trình ưu đãi. Với 
sản phẩm bán mới, chính sách thu đổi định hướng theo giá thị trường tại thời điểm 
giao dịch, thay cho tỷ lệ cố định trên giá trị hóa đơn, nhằm hạn chế tiếp tục tích lũy 
nghĩa vụ tiềm tàng trong tương lai theo cơ chế cũ.

Phương án bổ sung vốn và tái cơ cấu hoạt động kinh doanh 
— Đại hội thông qua phương án chào bán tối đa 550 triệu cổ phiếu. Nguồn vốn dự kiến 
phân bổ 60% cho kế hoạch kinh doanh gắn với điều chỉnh chính sách thu đổi, 20% bổ 
sung vốn lưu động và phần còn lại bổ sung vốn lưu động và/hoặc trả vay ngắn hạn. 
Nhu cầu vốn Q4/2026 và Q1/2027 lần lượt khoảng 3,100 tỷ VND và 2,800 tỷ VND. Giá và 
khối lượng phát hành thực tế chưa được xác định tại đại hội.  
— Doanh nghiệp dự kiến đóng 25–30 cửa hàng và mở khoảng 10 cửa hàng (có chọn lọc) 
trong năm 2026, tương ứng giảm ròng 15–20 cửa hàng; đồng thời đặt mục tiêu giảm 
20–30% chi phí nhân sự. Cổ tức tiền mặt năm 2026 được điều chỉnh về 0%; cổ tức 2025 
về 10% mệnh giá và đã chi trả. 
— 100% mẫu kim cương được AIGS và Vinacontrol kiểm định độc lập là kim cương thiên 
nhiên, phù hợp thông số 4C. PNJ dự kiến trang bị máy kiểm định tại các cửa hàng trọng 
điểm để khách hàng kiểm tra trực tiếp.

Đánh giá của KBSV 
— KBSV cho rằng huy động vốn từ phát hành riêng lẻ là phương án phù hợp ở thời điểm 
hiện tại trong bối cảnh lãi suất tăng cao, giúp PNJ cân đối thanh khoản và duy trì hoạt 
động liên tục. Tuy nhiên, với khối lượng phát hành lớn và chưa có phương án chi tiết, 
doanh nghiệp sẽ cần thêm thời gian để hoàn thành phương án phát hành và cân đối 
giữa lợi ích của cổ đông và doanh nghiệp.  
— Nhu cầu yếu tại Trung Quốc và cạnh tranh từ kim cương nuôi cấy giá thấp tiếp tục gây 
áp lực lên giá kim cương tự nhiên, khiến PNJ gặp khó khăn trong việc tiêu thụ tồn kho 
nhóm kim cương  và gia tăng tổn thất khi mua lại theo chính sách cũ. Thực tế, tỷ trọng 
kim cương và trang sức kim cương trong tổng tồn kho tăng từ 41% cuối Q2/2026 lên 
52% cuối Q3/2026, với giá trị ước khoảng 9,000 tỷ VND. KBSV cho rằng cần theo dõi (1) 
nhu cầu thị trường trong giai đoạn cuối năm; (2) tốc độ giảm tồn kho và dòng tiền thu 
về từ bán hàng; và (3) giá trị khách hàng bán lại. Diễn biến các yếu tố này sẽ quyết định 
khả năng thu hồi vốn, mức độ phục hồi biên lợi nhuận và nhu cầu trích lập bổ sung 
trong quá trình xử lý. 
— Ước tính bán niên sử dụng tỷ lệ tổn thất 20.3% đối với hàng bán trong giai đoạn 2020–
2026 dự kiến được thu mua lại, thấp hơn mức 28.9% trên các giao dịch mua lại thực tế 
từ 01/07–03/09/2026. Vì vậy, bên cạnh giả định thu đổi 80%, mức tổn thất khi xử lý 
hàng mua lại là yếu tố quan trọng cần theo dõi. Việc chuyển hạch toán dự phòng từ 
SG&A sang giá vốn tại kỳ soát xét bán niên cũng cho thấy áp lực biên lợi nhuận bị co 
hẹp trong quá trình tái cấu trúc về mặt kế toán.

## Document 311: Cyber Glitches, Bootlegged Chips & Deflating China

Source PDF: all report pdf/Cyber Glitches, Bootlegged Chips & Deflating China.pdf
Report source: researchmail轉寄
Date: 2026-11-17
Pages: 12
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

From:    Yardeni QuickTakes <yardeni-research@ghost.io>
Subject: Cyber Glitches, Bootlegged Chips & Deflating China
Date:    8 hours ago
Cyber Glitches, Bootlegged Chips & Deflating China

YARDENI QUICKTAKES
Cyber Glitches, Bootlegged
Chips & Deflating China
By Ed Yardeni & Elias Griepentrog • 16 Nov 2026
View in browser
Photo by Tonik / Unsplash

I. Cyber Glitches & Chip Controls
"Once is happenstance. Twice is coincidence. Three times is enemy
action." That's according to Ian Fleming’s Goldfinger (1959)

### Page 2

What, then, should we make of the recent string of technology
disruptions? In October, hackers tampered with freezers at roughly 700
French supermarkets, spoiling food and triggering fires. Last Thursday,
Microsoft suffered a 10-hour outage affecting Outlook and Teams. On
Friday, the Federal Aviation Administration (FAA) imposed the longest
nationwide flight ground stop since 9/11. Then, today, customers at major
US banks, including JPMorgan Chase, reported problems accessing their

accounts, while a computer failure temporarily grounded United Airlines
flights.
France blamed Iran's Islamic Revolutionary Guard Corps for the
supermarket attacks, although Tehran denies responsibility. The other
disruptions appear to have been technical failures, including software
bugs at Microsoft and JPMorgan Chase and a malfunctioning router at the
FAA. United's outage was resolved within 90 minutes. No evidence links
these incidents, but their timing is curious, to say the least.

Regulatory loopholes might also be considered glitches, and one may
finally be getting fixed. On Monday, the US Commerce Department
announced tighter restrictions to prevent Chinese companies from
accessing American AI chips and computing power through overseas data
centers, particularly in Southeast Asia. The measures follow scrutiny of
Nvidia's ties to Singapore-based Megaspeed, which has been accused of
circumventing US export controls.
The announcement added to the semiconductor stock selloff as investors

worried about lost chip sales and slower overseas data center investment.
The Philadelphia Semiconductor Index (SOX) plunged 5.3% today, while
the iShares Semiconductor ETF (SOXX) fell 5.4%. Nvidia declined 4.1%,
AMD 5.6%, and Micron more than 7%. The Nasdaq Composite dropped
2.5%, remaining below its 200-day moving average (charts).

### Page 3

Investors are particularly worried about Nvidia. How much of its revenue
could tighter export controls put at risk? Will CEO Jensen Huang's
departure unravel Nvidia's circular financing strategy and the broader AI
investment boom? Tomorrow's earnings call, led by interim CEO Colette
Kress, could provide crucial answers.
In any event, money appears to be rotating out of technology and into the
broader market. The S&P 500 equal-weight stock index has outperformed

its market-weight counterpart in recent weeks (chart). The former remains
near its recent record high, while the latter is down 5.1% from its record
high on October 5. The Nasdaq is 11.2% below its October 5 record high.
SOXX is down 35.3% from its June 22 record high. The market is holding
up remarkably well given the shellacking of the semiconductor stocks.

### Page 4

II. China Is Weakening
China's economy continues to lose steam, with domestic demand
particularly weak. Consumers are depressed. Deflation is mounting
because of excess production. Here’s more:
(1) Retail sales & production. Retail sales rose just 0.4% y/y in October.
Inflation-adjusted retail sales fell 0.4%, highlighting persistent weakness in
domestic demand. Both measures are hovering around zero, a sharp
contrast to the double-digit growth rates that prevailed before the

pandemic.
Chinese consumers remain deeply pessimistic, with the consumer
confidence index far below its historical average (chart). They've been hit

### Page 5

by a huge negative wealth effect in the property market. They've also
been losing money in the stock and gold markets this year.
Industrial production rose 4.8% y/y in October despite declining real retail
sales, highlighting the imbalance between China's manufacturing output
and domestic consumption that pushes Chinese producers to dump more
goods onto global markets (chart).
Automobile production remains near record highs at an annualized 27.9
million vehicles, despite weak domestic demand (chart). Total domestic

passenger vehicle retail sales over the trailing 12-month period through
October came to roughly 19.5 million units, down about 20% y/y. The glut
has been exported.

### Page 6

(2) Inflation. China's inflation data further confirm weak domestic demand
and excess production. Headline CPI rose just 0.7% y/y in October, while
prices excluding food and energy declined 0.4% y/y (chart).
The prolonged property downturn is a major drag on Chinese consumers.
New house prices across 70 cities fell 2.4% y/y in October (chart). New
house prices have been falling since 2022.

### Page 7

Financial markets reflect these challenges. Chinese real estate stocks
remain deeply depressed, with the Shenzhen Real Estate Index near
levels last seen during the global financial crisis (chart). The CSI 300 has
been volatile and trendless for years.
(3) Trade. China's manufacturing overcapacity is increasingly spilling into
global markets. Merchandise exports have surged to a record $4.2 trillion
annualized, far outpacing imports of $3.1 trillion (chart).

### Page 8

As a result, China's trade surplus reached a record $1.27 trillion over the
past 12 months (chart).
China's crude oil imports, adjusted for Brent prices, are flattening after
decades of growth (chart). That's largely because more Chinese are
driving electric vehicles. This development was weighing on global oil
prices until the war in the Middle East boosted them this year.

### Page 9

China's exporters are increasingly looking beyond the US, with shipments
to Europe surging to nearly $890 billion over the past year, almost twice
the $463 billion shipped to America (chart).
Exports to Vietnam have also soared, to a record $250 billion, alongside
strong growth in shipments to South Korea and India (chart).

### Page 10

(4) Money & credit. China's credit boom has left its banking system with
enormous outstanding loans of $41.9 trillion, almost three times the $14.2
trillion outstanding at US banks (chart).
Bank loan growth continues to slow, with bank loans and M2 growth rates
in clear downward trends (chart).

### Page 11

Weak bank credit growth reflects the broader economic malaise, even as
Chinese interest rates remain exceptionally low (chart).
The People's Bank of China continues accumulating gold, reflecting its
efforts to diversify official reserves (chart).

### Page 12

clause.
Yardeni QuickTakes © 2026 – Unsubscribe

ADVANCED ECONOMIES
EUROZONE
Robust growth but jobs stall and trade deficit rises. Q3 GDP growth
was confirmed at a robust 0.4% q/q, but employment was flat for the
first time since the Covid crisis. In September, intra-EU exports jum -
ped 1.7% m/m and 8.8% y/y. Stronger import growth widened the EU’s

rolling 12-month trade deficit with the rest of the world to EUR63.3bn
(0.4% of GDP). These are both indications of success of the EU’s struc -
tural efforts to boost its single market and have a more domestic de -
mand-driven growth. That said, the bilateral deficit with China reached
a new high of EUR38.9bn.
A decision on ECB leadership succession could be reached as early
as next week when EU Leaders meet on 18 November with a pac -
kage deal including Pablo Hernandez de Cos as President, a yet-to-be

named German chief economist, and former French cabinet member
Laurence Boone (currently running Santander France CIB) in charge of
market operations.
Upcoming: September industrial production (Monday); deadline for
nominations to ECB Board vacancies (which should provide clarity on
the full package including the President) and ECB Financial Stability
Review (Wednesday); Q3 negotiated wages (Friday).
France
A string of modest improvements. Unemployment edged down 0.1pp

to 8.2% in Q3, with a broadly stable activity rate. The Banque de France
business survey pointed to “moderate” October growth, driven main -
ly by manufacturing and market services. This aligns with our now -
cast of 0.2% q/q in Q4 GDP growth. Business starts increased 12% y/y
(excluding micro-entrepreneurs), while corporate bankruptcies rose
4.4% y/y over the 12 months to September, with a stable economic
weight (for more context, see our latest dashboard Business creations

and bankruptcies in France). The government brought forward the mi-
nimum-wage increase to December 1 rst and set it at 1.6%. The Court
of Auditors has published a report outlining “quickly implementable”
measures that would yield savings of EUR 32.2bn (1% of GDP) in public
finances (EUR20.9bn in spending and EUR 11.3bn in revenues), inclu -
ding a freeze on social benefits and an increase in the intermediate VAT
rate. The OAT-bund spread fell below 150 bps for the first time since

mid-October, before rising back to 154 as global yields rebounded.
Upcoming: INSEE November business survey (Friday).
Germany
Contrasted export performance. September exports rose a stron -
ger-than-expected 2.0% m/m, but imports surged 4.2%, narrowing the
trade surplus to EUR15.6bn from EUR17.9bn. The export picture was
sharply divided geographically: shipments to the EU rose 3.5% m/m,
while those to the US, China and the UK declined. Final October infla -

tion data confirmed the slowdown from preliminary estimates. Whole-
sale prices increased by 0.4, down from 1.9% in September, and in line
with market estimates.
Upcoming: November GfK Consumer Confidence (Tuesday).
Italy
Industry rebounds and trade surplus swells. Industrial production beat
expectations by rising by 1.2% m/m in September, driven by energy and
capital goods, although overall output remained 0.1% lower than a year
earlier notably owing to intermediate goods (-1.4% y/y) and consumer

goods (-3.0% y/y). On a monthly basis, chemical output contracted the
most (-8.3% m/m). The trade surplus over January-September reached
EUR36.8bn, up 37.7% YTD, as exports grew 4.7% against 2.8% for im -
ports.
Upcoming: October final HICP (Tuesday).
UNITED KINGDOM
Growth accelerates to 0.7% q/q in Q3, up from 0.5% in Q2, led by a
0.9% expansion in services. Gross fixed-capital formation increased
1.7%, while household and government consumption together rose

0.6%. Business investment grew 0.4%, confounding expectations of a
contraction. Net exports contributed negatively, however, as imports
outpaced exports.
Upcoming: September unemployment and average earnings (Tues -
day); October inflation (Wednesday); UK-EU “Reset” Summit (Friday).
UNITED STATES
Hot inflation revives hawkish Fed bets. Headline and core CPI both sur-
prised to the upside in October, with headline prices rising 0.4% m/m
and core CPI posting a third consecutive 0.3% increase, a pace incons -

istent with a return to the 2% target in the near term. Producer-price
pressures remained even stronger, with headline and core PPI reaching
6.5% and 5.0% y/y respectively (below consensus for headline, in line
for core).
Fed Governor Waller said a 50bp hike “should be considered” if core
inflation came in hot, while Dallas Fed President Logan subsequent -
ly suggested the FOMC might need to move more rapidly this winter.
The two-year Treasury yield rose more than 30bp over the week to

4.85% on 12 November, while the 10Y had its worst week since Libe -
ration Day. Meanwhile, existing-home sales fell another 5.5% m/m as
higher mortgage rates weighed on housing demand.
Upcoming: October retail sales and industrial production (Tuesday);
FOMC minutes, October building permits and housing starts (Wednes-
day).
JAPAN
Stalling growth and December BoJ hike signaled. GDP growth came
in at 0.0% q/q (-0.1% AR) in Q3. This below-consensus print ended

a run of three quarters of solid growth (consensus: 0.1%, prior: 0.4%).
Household consumption fell again (-0.3% q/q) under the combined
effect of inflation and adverse weather. Net exports also weighed on
growth due to a marked rebound in imports (2.5% q/q, prior: -1.3%).
On the bright side, business investment bounced back strongly
(1.6% q/q) after contracting in both H1 quarters.
The October Monetary Policy Meeting summary suggests policyma -
kers no longer see a need to be “overly cautious” about rate hikes,

with underlying inflation described as having “already reached 2%”
and the need to move the policy rate towards neutral now clearer.
Price pressures remain significant: PPI inflation accelerated to 7.1%
y/y in October. September real household spending nevertheless fell
2.8% m/m despite solid wage momentum.
Upcoming: October CPI inflation (Friday).

economic-research.bnpparibas.comEcoWeek 26.42 / 16 November 2026

EMERGING ECONOMIES
ASIA
China
Weak domestic inflation, weak domestic demand, booming external
trade. CPI inflation slowed to 0.7% y/y in October and core inflation
to 0.8%. By contrast, producer price inflation continued to rise (rea -
ching 5.7% vs. 5.0% in September, driven by energy and chips), sug -
gesting margin compression for domestic consumer-facing businesses.

In October, export growth remained exceptionally strong at 35.2% y/y in
USD terms, supported by global demand for AI-related and green-tech
products. Integrated-circuit exports surged 137.1% in value, largely re-
flecting price effects, while auto exports rose 37.6%, primarily because
of higher volumes. Imports increased 41.6%, and the trade surplus
reached USD105.3bn in October and USD1,265bn over the 12 mon -
ths to October (from 1,180bn in 2025). Industrial production growth

slowed to 4.8% y/y in October from 5.5% in September, although the
electronics sector still posted solid performance. Meanwhile, domes -
tic demand remained weak, with both consumption and investment
slowing. Retail sales increased by 0.4% y/y in nominal terms (from
1.1%) and contracted in real terms, while the fall in nominal fixed-as -
set investment continued. The loss of momentum in economic growth
was also visible in services production growth (4.6% y/y vs. 4.8%).

India
Inflation breaks through 6%. Headline CPI accelerated to 6.1% y/y
in October from 5.5% previously, moving above the RBI’s 6% ceiling for
the first time in two years. Core inflation increased to 4.5%. With the
policy rate already raised 25bp to 5.5% in October, another 25bp in -
crease is expected at the December meeting.
CENTRAL EUROPE
Mild Q3 GDP uptick and mixed inflation data. The region’s Q3 GDP
growth was slightly higher compared to the previous quarter (2.2% y/y;

2.0% in Q2), mainly driven by Poland (3.6% y/y) and to a lesser extent
by Hungary (1.5% y/y) and Czech Rep. (1.6% y/y). Romania saw a po -
sitive growth (0.3% y/y) albeit soft, after three consecutive quarters in
contraction. Meanwhile, inflation print was mixed with an increase in
Hungary (2.0% y/y), Romania (6.9% y/y) and Poland (3.5% y/y). Inflation
was softer in Czech rep. (2.4% y/y) and Slovakia (3.0% y/y). Only Hun -
gary and Poland’s inflation rates are within the central bank’s target

range. Separately, Romania’s policy rate was kept on hold at 6.5% as
expected.
LATIN AMERICA
Persistent inflation pressures. In October, inflation rose in Brazil
(4.6% y/y), Mexico (3.8% y/y), and Colombia (6.3% y/y), driven by soa -
ring food prices, exacerbated by El Niño’s impact on agricultural out -
put. In Brazil, food inflation reached 5.7% y/y, while Mexico’s non-core
food prices jumped to 13.2% y/y, and Colombia’s food prices hit 7.3% y/y,

the highest since 2023. Central banks are now expected to maintain
rates on hold for an extended period.
Brazil
Modest fiscal adjustment. On 12 November, the Senate approved the
main provisions of a fiscal adjustment package aiming for a prima -
ry surplus of 0.5% of GDP in 2027. However, the proposed measures
are modest and do not address the structural rigidity of mandatory
spending. President Lula faces a difficult balancing act of reassuring
markets and preserving social commitments.

Paraguay
Ueno Bank collapse triggers confidence crisis. Paraguay’s sixth-largest
bank, Ueno Bank, was placed into resolution on 2nd November after its
capital fell below regulatory minimums, amid concerns over improper
related-party lending. The bank served nearly 3 million customers, re-
presenting around 60% of the adult population. Its collapse triggered a
major political crisis, culminating in President Santiago Pena’s removal
from office. No systemic banking crisis is expected, as other banks re -

main solvent and liquid. However, fiscal pressures are mounting, while
political uncertainty poses downside risks to growth. On November
11th, Fitch placed Paraguay’s (BB+) rating on Negative Watch, citing
risks to depositor confidence and fiscal stability.
COMMODITIES
Oil and gas: Physical pressures ease, but geopolitical risk lingers.
Brent rose by 3.4% over the week to USD96/bbl (after having fallen
below USD90 for a short while this week on hopes Iran-Us talks were

gaining traction). Physical supply tensions continued to ease: the IEA
reported that global crude supply exceeded demand in October for the
first time since the outbreak of the war and it expects the market to re-
turn to surplus in Q4. US commercial crude inventories rose by 4.7mb,
while strategic reserves fell by 2.6mb. Nevertheless, diesel cracks have
remained almost stable over the week (0.2% in the US and 0.4% in
Europe) on the back of persisting limited exports from the Gulf and

damages on Russian refineries. European gas prices rose 8% to EUR89/
MWh amid continuing constraints on Gulf LNG exports, colder weather
and outages at Norwegian gas facilities.
TO WATCH
Trilateral Summit between presidents Putin, Trump and Xi on the side-
lines of APEC Summit (Thu).

### Page 2

2
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
All state Housing Sentiment Indexes are at weak levels.
However, November saw a bigger decline in Western
Australia (–4.8%mth) which now has the weakest housing-
related sentiment index across the major states at just 80.9.
Queensland and South Australia moved in line with the

national index. Moves were notably milder in New South
Wales (–1.7%) and especially Victoria (–0.7%) where the state
index, at 86.6, is the least pessimistic of the major states.
The sub-group detail showed a more pronounced weakening in
housing-related sentiment amongst high income earners and
consumers aged over 55 with sentiment holding about steady
amongst low-income earners, albeit at weaker overall levels.
Our November consumer sentiment survey also included

two new regular questions on consumers’ property buying
and selling intentions over the next twelve months. These
questions will continue to be run every month.
Despite the broader pessimism around housing, a relatively
high number of consumers are planning to buy in 2027.
Responses show 12.8% of consumers plan to buy a new
home over the next year. Comparable figures from Westpac’s
Home Ownership survey going back to 2019 suggest this is a
relatively firm result, up slightly since the middle of the year,

on par with the start of 2025 and above the levels reported
during the COVID pandemic in 2020-21. The result suggests
there is significant ‘latent demand’ for home purchases in
Australia, and that many buyers are just waiting for the right
opportunity. The state detail shows intentions are particularly
high in Queensland where one in five consumers is reportedly
planning to buy in 2027.
Interestingly, the positive intentions are not just around home

purchase with 11.2% of consumers reporting that they plan
to buy an investment property over the next year. Again,
comparable figures from Westpac’s Home Ownership survey
show this is up a tick compared to May and only marginally
below the 11.5% reporting intentions to buy investment
properties back in January 2025.
BULLETIN
The Westpac Housing Sentiment Index fell 2.2% to 83.2 in
November from 85.3 in October.
The Index is a composite based on housing-related sentiment

measures from the Westpac-Melbourne Institute Consumer
Sentiment survey. It already appears regularly in Westpac’s
Housing Pulse reports but will now be published on a
monthly basis. The Index has proven to be an extremely useful
predictor of turning points in housing markets, both nationally
and across states. Westpac is also introducing two new
survey questions on consumers property buying and selling
intentions that will be run every month.
The November update shows housing-related sentiment

remains remain deeply pessimistic. At 85.3, the latest Index
read is in the bottom 3% of observations since 2005, when all
components first became available. That said, it is still above the
81.4 low in late 2022 and the 78.6 registered when the COVID
pandemic hit in 2020. Housing-related sentiment was markedly
weaker during the slump in the late 1980s/early 1990s.
Component-wise, the November sentiment decline centred on
a sharp downgrade to price expectations. As detailed below,

the Westpac Housing Sentiment Index composite is based
on four sub-indexes: one tracking assessments of ‘time to buy
a dwelling’; one tracking ‘consumer house price expectations’;
one tracking consumer expectations for unemployment and a
measure of risk aversion based on consumer assessments of
the ‘wisest place for savings’.
Consumer price expectations took a big step down in
November. The Westpac–Melbourne Institute Index of
House Price Expectations recorded a steep 13.4% drop

to 99.6, marking the second biggest monthly deterioration
since the COVID period and the first net negative (sub-100)
read since November 2022. The detailed responses show
consumers are now evenly split on the outlook, just over 38%
expecting more declines, 19% expecting no change and 38%
expecting a rise (5% reported “don’t know).
At least some consumers are starting to see lower prices as
a potential buying opportunity. The ‘time to buy a dwelling’

index rose 1.3% to 89.6 in November, to be up 1.3% on a year
ago as well but still at a very weak level overall, well below
the long run average of 114.
The wider economic backdrop was also less conducive for
housing-related sentiment. Consumers continue to report
a gradual rise in job-loss fears and risk aversion remains
elevated near historic highs. Both factors are likely to be
deterring prospective home buyers.
Housing sentiment sinks but buying intentions positive

“Despite the broader pessimism
around housing, a relatively high
number of consumers are planning
to buy ...”
Matthew Hassan
Head of Australian Macro-Forecasting

### Page 3

Past performance is not a reliable indicator of future performance. The forecasts given above are predictive in character. Whilst every effort has been taken to ensure that the assumptions on which the forecasts are based are reasonable, 
the forecasts may be affected by incorrect assumptions or by known or unknown risks and uncertainties. The results ultimately achieved may differ substantially from these forecasts.
3
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
BULLETIN
Conversely, just 8.2% of Australians are planning to sell a home 
over the next year. Comparable figures from Westpac’s Home 
Ownership survey suggest this is down significantly from 10.3% 
back in May and near the 7.7% low recorded at the start of 2024. 
On investment properties, 6.7% of all those surveyed reported 
plans to sell. However, only 23% of consumers reported 
owning an investment property – as a share of this sub-
group, the proportion planning to sell is just over 26%. While 
it is unclear whether this is relatively high (no comparable 
measures are available from Westpac’s Home Ownership 
survey), a third of this same sub-group reported an intention to 
buy an investment property over the next year, indicating that 
amongst investment property owners prospective buyers still 
outnumber prospective sellers. 
Overall, the consumer views on housing show a clear tension. 
On the one side, high interest rates, a continued slide in prices 
and unease about a riskier outlook for jobs and the wider 
economy are weighing heavily on housing-related sentiment. 
But against this, relatively few are planning to sell and there 
is a relatively high proportion of consumers looking to buy. 
This suggests that conditions could recover quite quickly if and 
when the macro-economic backdrop becomes less threatening 
and the market starts to stabilise.

The survey is conducted by OZINFO & DYNATA. Respondents are selected at random. This latest survey is based on 1200 adults aged 18 years and over, across Australia. It was conducted in the week from 
2 November to 6 November.  The data have been weighted to reflect Australia’s population distribution. Copyright at all times remains with the Melbourne Institute of Applied Economic and Social Research.
About the Westpac Housing Sentiment Index 
The Westpac Housing Sentiment Index is a composite 
measure based on a weighted combination of four indexes 
from the Westpac–Melbourne Institute Consumer 
Sentiment survey. Two of these are ‘primary’ components 
with a higher weight that relate directly to consumer 
perceptions of housing market conditions: the Westpac–
Melbourne Institute ‘time to buy a dwelling’ index and the 
Westpac–Melbourne Institute House Price Expectations 
Index. The remaining ‘supplementary’ components, with 
lower weights, relate to consumer assessments of job 
security – the Westpac–Melbourne Institute Unemployment 
Expectations Index – and risk appetite – the Westpac Risk 
Aversion Index.
Each of these components is seasonally adjusted, converted 
to a consistent base and combined using fixed weights 
determined by historical regression analysis. Note that the 
house price expectations component is only available from 
2009 on – a re-weighted composite based on the remaining 
measures is used for earlier periods.  
The resulting composite measures provide significant insight 
into housing market conditions both nationally and at the 
individual state level. The national index has over 50yrs of 
history and a clear lead indicator relationship with a variety 
of housing market metrics. The index is particularly good at 
picking turning points in housing market turnover – correctly 
anticipating every major upswing and downturn since 1980 
with a lead of around three months (four once the timeliness 
of sentiment updates is included).

## Document 382: SpaceX完成320亿美元AI芯片债券发行，收益率最高超过8%

Source PDF: all report pdf/SpaceX完成320亿美元AI芯片债券发行，收益率最高超过8%.pdf
Report source: WallstreetCN (Global)
Date: 2026-11-20
Pages: 2
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

SpaceX 通过特殊⽬的载体完成 320 亿美元债券发⾏，为采购英伟达最新⼀代 Rubin 芯⽚融
资，最⼤⼀档票据收益率达 8.259% 。此次表外融资旨在维护投资级评级，资⾦将⽤于购买
约 473 亿美元 GPU 设备。⾼融资收益率反映出投资者对芯⽚资产估值及技术迭代⻛险的审
慎态度。
摘要：
SpaceX 正通过资本市场为 AI 基础设施扩张筹集巨额资⾦。公司完成 320 亿美元债券发⾏，为⼤
规模采购英伟达最新⼀代 AI 芯⽚提供资⾦。
据彭博援引知情⼈⼠，此次债券于周四分三档完成定价，由⾼盛集团和 Apollo Global
Management 主导承销。其中，规模最⼤的 A3 级票据约 252 亿美元，定价收益率⾼达 8.259% ，
显著⾼于同期美国投资级债券 5.95% 的平均收益率。
此次融资通过特殊⽬的载体（ SPV ）发⾏，旨在将相关债务置于 SpaceX 资产负债表之外，以维
护公司的投资级信⽤评级。 SpaceX ⾸席财务官 Bret Johnsen 此前在投资者电话会议上强调，公

司拥有 “ 强劲的投资级资产负债表 ” 。
较⾼的融资收益率则反映出投资者对这⼀新型 AI 芯⽚融资结构的审慎态度。随着科技公司竞相
建设 AI 基础设施，如何在控制资产负债表压⼒的同时筹集巨额资⾦，正成为资本市场的新课题。
表外融资结构旨在维护投资级评级
此次债券通过名为 Saturn Funding Trust 的特殊⽬的载体发⾏，由 Apollo 旗下实体管理。 SpaceX
以租赁⽅式使⽤载体持有的芯⽚，租期约五年，并承诺即使设备发⽣故障或遭遇⾃然灾害，仍
须继续履⾏付款义务。
这⼀结构旨在将相关融资债务留在 SpaceX 资产负债表之外，降低对公司信⽤指标的影响。对于
需要持续投⼊巨额资⾦的科技公司⽽⾔，投资级评级意味着能够以更有利的条件获取融资。
SpaceX 今年 6 ⽉完成 862 亿美元 IPO ，随后⼜发⾏了 250 亿美元投资级公司债。不过，此前发⾏
的⽆担保债券在⼆级市场表现不佳，也令部分投资者对这笔新交易⼼存疑虑。
⾼收益率吸引传统投资者⼊场
此次发⾏中，约 260 亿美元债券已获得投资者提前认购，周四最终发售的 60 亿美元为 A3 级票据

的剩余部分。⻉莱德、⾼盛资产管理和 KKR 旗下账⼾均参与其中，成为锚定投资者。
CreditSights 科技、媒体及电信研究主管 Davis Hebert 表⽰， SpaceX 此前发⾏的⽆担保债券令投
资者蒙受损失，市场⼀度对这笔交易能否顺利完成持怀疑态度。他认为，较⾼的收益率正是吸
引传统投资者参与、补偿相关⻛险的重要条件。
对于传统投资级债券投资者⽽⾔，此次交易结构较为复杂。尤其是与 AI GPU 挂钩的融资仍属新
型交易，芯⽚⽣命周期、技术迭代及资产估值等⻛险，都增加了投资者的评估难度。
融资资⾦剑指英伟达 Rubin 芯⽚

### Page 2

The Week Ahead November 6, 2026 
                                                                                                                                         2

FORTHCOMING DATA 
RELEASES

Taiwan

Export growth is expected to ease to 
44.4% yoy in October from 60.9% in 
September. A higher comparison base 
makes exceptional export growth 
difficult to sustain from 4Q onwards. The 
global AI cycle is also expected to settle 
into a more normal pace into 2027, as 
higher interest rates lift capital costs 
and US mid-term election results 
heighten regulatory uncertainty. These 
factors underpin our forecast for 
Taiwan’s GDP growth to return to high 
single digits from 4Q onwards and into 
2027.

Malaysia

We expect Malaysia’s industrial 
production growth to rise to 5.5% yoy in 
September 2026 from 5.0% yoy in 
August. The uptick was likely driven by 
export-oriented manufacturing as 
strong artificial intelligence (AI)-related 
demand continued to support growth in 
electrical and electronics goods, 
alongside a smaller contraction in mining.

China

Export growth is expected to decelerate 
from 36.3% yoy in September to 28.1% in 
October. Both the official manufacturing 
PMI and new export orders sub-index fell 
below 50 after two months in expansion 
territory. High-frequency trade indicators 
also point to slowing momentum, with 
average daily deadweight tonnage handled 
at China’s 20 major ports falling from 7.2% 
yoy in September to -4.2% in October. 
Freight activity weakened as well, with 
growth in international cargo flights 
dropping from 3.6% yoy to -5.9% over the 
same period. On the price front, factory-gate 
and raw material price pressures moderated, 
with the main raw material purchasing price 
and producer price sub-indices declining 
from 60.8 and 54.0 to 56.8 and 51.7, 
respectively. Despite remaining above the 
50 threshold, the moderation suggests some 
easing in pipeline price pressures. 
Downstream consumer price pressures are 
also likely to remain subdued amid soft 
domestic demand. As a result, CPI inflation 
is expected to ease slightly from 0.9% yoy in 
September to 0.8% in October.

India

We expect headline inflation to rise to 6.0% 
yoy in October from 5.5% the month before, 
on higher food costs and a low base effect. 
High frequency data pointed to a further 
pick-up in sequential pressures in food 
segments, across cereals, edible oil, 
perishables (vegetables) etc. Non-food 
components should find relief from further 
moderation in precious metals and delay in 
passage of retail fuel price changes. Core 
inflation is nonetheless set to quicken to 4.5-
4.6% as services notch increases and as 
indirect tax effects in the base, fall out of the 
equation. With inflation expected to test 
past the higher end of the RBI target range, 
we expect a second successive rate hike in 
December. The decline in oil prices has also 
led to cooling of overnight OIS rates as 
concerns over aggressive rate hikes have 
eased. Goods trade deficit is likely to stay 
wide at above $30bn in Oct.

## Document 402: Top of Mind - Assessing a Less Transparent Fed

Source PDF: all report pdf/Top of Mind - Assessing a Less Transparent Fed.pdf
Report source: Goldman Sachs (Top of Mind)
Date: 2026-08-25
Pages: 26
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

The Goldman Sachs Group, Inc.
Fed Chairman Kevin Warsh is steering the Fed into a less transparent era. So, is that
a positive or negative development, and what could it mean for markets and the
economy? We speak with Former Fed governors Donald Kohn and Stephen Miran
as well as GS’ Jan Hatzius who agree that rigid “Odyssean” forward guidance that
commits policymakers to a pre-set path risks policy mistakes and is therefore rarely
appropriate. But they somewhat disagree about the benefits of great er transparency

around the Fed’s reaction function. Hatzius and Kohn argue that such transparency
helps markets better anticipate and price in policy changes, accelerating monetary
policy transmission. But Miran believes that the line between committing to a path
and being transparent is clearer in theory than in practice. And while GS economists
and strategists find that reduc ed transparency leads to increased market volatility,
the question is whether this just increases the market’s noise or also improves the

market’s signal. Hatzius argues largely for the former and Miran for the latter.
Jan Hatzius, Chief Economist and Head of Global Investment Research, Goldman Sachs
“Markets price what they think the Fed will do, not what they think the Fed should do. That will remain true even if the Fed
obscures its reaction function… market guesses about the Fed’s next moves will simply become worse, leading to more
volatility... and importantly, volatility that serves no constructive economic purpose.”

Donald Kohn, former Vice Chairman of the Board of Governors of the Federal Reserve System, Robert V. Roosa
Chair in International Economics and Senior Fellow, Brookings Institution
“I sympathize with Chairman Warsh to some extent. [But] the real problem with Warsh’s approach is his reluctance to articulate
how the FOMC sees the economy evolving… explaining the Fed’s thinking about the economic outlook is essential in helping
markets act as a stabilizing force and is a core component of accountability.”
